<meta>This page continues the discussion of the landscape surrounding Stonehenge, specifically focusing on the Cursus (the ancient racecourse) and its mathematical relationship to the main monument. The previous page likely ended with a description of the British charioteers' skill.</meta>

### STONEHENGE

...that skill, spoken of by *Caesar*. <note>Julius Caesar described the impressive ability of British charioteers to stay balanced and steer even on steep slopes.</note> But the exquisite softness of the turf prevents any great damage by a fall. The ground hereabouts declines somewhat northward. The main part of this <term>hippodrome<gloss>an ancient stadium for horse and chariot racing</gloss></term> is upon a gentle ridge running east and west. This rendered the place cooler.

<margin>Plate 29</margin>
On the southern ridge, toward the west end of it, are many considerable <term>barrows<gloss>ancient burial mounds made of earth and stones</gloss></term>: but none towards the east end, for that would obstruct the view of *Stonehenge*. There are many barrows, but of no considerable bulk, on the north side, upon the extensive ascent, toward the great north long barrow. This magnificent work of the <term>cursus<gloss>a long, narrow Neolithic earthwork enclosure</gloss></term> is drawn due east and west: except a small variation of 4 or 5 degrees southward from the east. If we measure along the bank, from the eastern <term>turning post<gloss>original Latin: "meta," the goal or turning point in a race</gloss></term>, at exactly 700 <term>cubits<gloss>an ancient unit of length based on the forearm, roughly 18 to 20 inches</gloss></term>, we come over against the middle line of the straight part of the avenue to *Stonehenge*: 500 cubits further conducts us to the gaps or opposite entrances, I before mentioned; which we suppose functioned as distance posts. The whole interval between the eastern turning post and these gaps is 1200 cubits. At 1000 cubits more, we come to the place where the west wing of the avenue enters the southern ditch of the *cursus*. That west wing too, is just 1000 cubits long to its union with the straight part of the *Stonehenge* avenue.

<margin>Plate 28</margin>
Likewise, the straight part of the *Stonehenge* avenue is just 1000 cubits long, as mentioned in its proper place.

This west wing begins in the bottom of that valley which crosses the middle of the *cursus* and, sweeping along by the bottom of the hill in a gentle curve, meets with the lower end of the straight part of the *Stonehenge* avenue, where the wing or avenue unites to it at an equal angle. So that the whole work is laid out with great judgment and symmetry; and curiously adapted to the ground, which was well considered before the plot was marked out by the first surveyors.

<margin>Plate 30</margin>
From the bottom of the valley crossing the middle of the *cursus* to the western turning post is 3800 cubits more, making in the whole 6000 cubits. The north end of the eastern turning post does not extend as far as the northern bank of the *cursus*: I suppose the reason is so that there might be liberty in that direction to stop the horses at the end of the course. Therefore they set out on the south side of the *cursus* and returned by the north side. I observe the ditch and bank towards the eastern end of the *cursus* much obscured by the trampling of men and horses frequenting the spectacles here; this being the most thronged area.

The *Cursus* is directly north from *Stonehenge*: so exactly, that the meridian line <note>The north-south line passing through a specific location.</note> of *Stonehenge* passes precisely through the middle of the *Cursus*. And when we stand in the grand entrance of *Stonehenge* and observe the two extremities of the *Cursus*—the eastern and western turning posts—they are each exactly 60 degrees from the meridian line on each hand, making a third part of the circle of the horizon. By which we see the Druids well understood the geometry of a circle and its measure of 360 parts.

Pausanias <note>A 2nd-century AD Greek traveler and geographer.</note> in his *Description of Greece (Boeotia)* says, "among the *Thebans*, by the *Pratis* gate is the *Gymnasium* of *Iolaus* and likewise the *stadium*, which is a bank of earth thrown up, such as that at *Olympia* and those of the *Laurii*. In the same place is the heroical monument of *Iolaus*. A little beyond, to the right, is the *hippodrome*, and in it *Pindar*’s monument." The same author in his eighth book (*Arcadia*) writes, "that before the walls of *Mantinea*, in a field, was a *stadium* made for horse-races in honor of *Antinous*. Not far from it was the temple of *Neptune the Equestrian* and others." So that we see it was the manner of the ancient *Greeks* thus to define their places for sports by banks of earth, and to place them near their temples.

After the *Romans* had borrowed the use of the *British* chariots for traveling and the like, they used them too in the *Circensian* games. <note>Public games and races held in the Roman circus.</note> Thus *Sidonius Apollinaris* <note>A 5th-century poet and diplomat.</note> wrote in his poem upon it (Book 22):

->At once the drivers urge them on with their whips,<-
<note>original: "Instant verberibus simul regentes,"</note>
->And now, leaning forward from the scythed chariot,<-
<note>original: "Jamque et pectora prona de covinno"</note>
->They are swept away at full speed.<-
<note>original: "Extensi rapiuntur."</note>

He highlights the alignment of the Cursus with Stonehenge and notes the mathematical sophistication involved in its layout.</summary>

that dexterity, spoken of by *Caesar*. <note>Referencing Julius Caesar’s accounts of the skill of British chariot drivers.</note> But the exquisite softness of the turf prevents any great damage by a fall. After the *Romans* had borrowed the use of the *British* chariots for traveling and the like, they used them too in the *Circensian* games <note>Public games held in a Roman circus, primarily chariot races.</note>. ->At once the drivers urge them with whips,<-

# STONEHENGE

as well as different orders and stations in them. 

<margin>Plate 24</margin>
Most of the barrows <note>burial mounds</note> have little ditches around, extremely well defined. In many is a circular ditch 60 cubits <note>A cubit is an ancient unit of length based on the forearm, roughly 18 inches or 45 centimeters.</note> in diameter, with a very small *burial mound* <note>original Latin: *tumulus*</note> in the center. 60 or even 100 cubits is a very common diameter in the large barrows. Often, they are set in rows, and equidistant, so as to produce a regular and pretty appearance, and with some particular regard to the parts of the temple, the avenues, or the *racecourse* <note>original Latin: *cursus*, referring to a large Neolithic earthwork enclosure near Stonehenge that resembles a track</note>. For instance, where the avenue begins at the first elevation, from *Radfin* Ford, advancing towards *Stonehenge*, seven large and flat old barrows are on the right hand of the avenue, towards the east end of the *racecourse*, seven large barrows of a newer shape, are on the left hand: both these groups before spoken of, are placed in a similar manner, in regard to the avenue, and as wings or openings to it. 

<margin>Plate 33</margin>
Upon every range of hills, quite round *Stonehenge*, are successive groups of barrows, for some miles: and we may even observe, that great barrow by Lord *Pembroke's* park at *Wilton*, which I call the tomb of *Carvilius* <note>Carvilius was one of the four kings of Kent who fought against Julius Caesar; Stukeley identified this mound with him based on local lore</note>, is set within view of *Stonehenge*.

In 1722, my late Lord *Pembroke*, Earl *Thomas*, who was pleased to favor my inquiries at this place, opened a barrow, in order to find the position of the body observed in these early days. He pitched upon one of those south of *Stonehenge*, close upon the road thither from *Wilton*: and on the east side of the road. It is one of the double barrows, or where two are enclosed in one ditch: one of those, which I suppose the later kind, and of a fine 

<margin>Plate 9</margin>
turned bell-fashion. It may be seen in Plate IX. On the west side, he made a section from the top to the bottom, an entire segment, from center to circumference. The manner of composition of the barrow was good earth, quite through, except a coat of chalk of about two foot thickness, covering it quite over, under the turf. Hence it appears, that the method of making these barrows was to dig up the turf for a great space round, till the barrow was brought to its intended bulk. Then with the chalk, dug out of the environing ditch, they powdered it all over. So that for a considerable time, these barrows must have looked white: even for some number of years. And the notion of sanctity annexed to them, forbid people trampling on them, till perfectly settled and turfed over. Hence the neatness of their form to this day. At the top or center of this barrow, not above three foot under the surface, my Lord found the skeleton of the interred; perfect, of a reasonable size, the head lying toward *Stonehenge*, or northward.

The year following, in order to prosecute this inquiry, by my Lord's order, I began upon a barrow north of *Stonehenge*, in that group south of the *racecourse*. It is one of the double barrows there: and the more easterly, and lower of the two: likewise somewhat less. It was reasonable to believe, this was the *burial place* <note>original: *sepulture*</note> of a man and his wife: and that the lesser was the female: and so it proved, at least a daughter. We made a large cut on the top from east to west. After the turf taken off, we came to the layer of chalk, as before, then fine garden mould <note>rich, fertile topsoil</note>. About three foot below the surface, a layer of flints, following the curve <note>original: "humouring the convexity"</note> of the barrow. These flints are gathered from the surface of the downs in some places, especially where it has been plowed. This being about a foot thick, rested on a layer of soft mould another foot: in which was enclosed an urn full of bones. This urn was of unbaked clay, of a dark reddish colour: crumbled into pieces. It had been rudely wrought with small mouldings round the verge, and other circular channels on the outside, with several indentures 

<margin>Plate 32</margin>
between, made with a pointed tool, as depicted in Plate XXXII. where I have drawn all the sorts of things found in this barrow. The bones had been burnt, and crowded all together in a little heap, not so much as a hat crown would contain. The collar bone, and one side of the under-jaw are engraved in their true magnitude. It appears to have been a girl of about 14 years old, by their bulk and the great quantity of female ornaments mixed

mixed with the bones, all of which we gathered. Beads of all sorts, and in great number, of glass of diverse colors, most yellow, one black. Many single, many in long pieces notched between, so as to resemble a string of beads, and these were generally of a blue color. There were many of amber, of all shapes and sizes: flat squares, long squares, round, oblong, little and great. Likewise many of earth, of different shapes, magnitude, and color: some little and white, many large and flattish like a button, others like a pulley. But all had holes to run a string through, either through their diameter or sides. Many of the button sort seem to have been covered with metal, there being a rim worked in them, wherein to turn the edge of the covering. One of these was covered with a thin film of pure gold. These were the young lady's ornaments. And had all undergone the fire: so that what would easily consume fell to pieces as soon as handled. Much of the amber burnt half through. This person was a heroine, for we found the head of her javelin in brass. At the bottom are two holes for the pins that fastened it to the staff. Besides, there was a sharp bodkin <note>A small, pointed tool for piercing holes in fabric or leather.</note>, round at one end, square at the other, where it went into a handle. I still preserve whatever is permanent of these trinkets. But we recomposed the ashes of the illustrious defunct <note>The deceased person.</note>, and covered them with earth. Leaving visible marks at the top of the barrow having been opened, to dissuade any other from again disturbing them: and this was our practice in all the rest.

Then we opened the next barrow to it, enclosed in the same ditch, which we supposed belonged to the husband or father of this lady. At fourteen inches deep, the mould being mixed with chalk, we came to the entire skeleton of a man. The skull and all the bones were exceedingly rotten and perished through length of time. Though this was a barrow of the latest sort, as we conjecture. The body lay north and south, the head to the north, as that Lord *Pembroke* <note>Thomas Herbert, 8th Earl of Pembroke (1656–1733), a nobleman and collector who conducted early excavations at the site.</note> opened.

Next, I went westward, to a group of barrows whence *Stonehenge* bears east north-east. Here is a large barrow ditched about, but of an ancient make. On that side next *Stonehenge* are ten lesser, small, and as it were crowded together. South of the great one is another barrow, larger than those of the group, but not equaling the first. It would seem that a man and his wife were buried in the two larger, and that the rest were of their children or dependants. One of the small ones, 20 <term>cubits<gloss>An ancient unit of measure; Stukeley believed the "Druid's cubit" used here was about 20.8 inches.</gloss></term> in diameter, I cut through, with a pit nine foot in diameter, to the surface of the natural chalk, in the center of the barrow; where was a little hole cut. A child's body (as it seems) had been burnt here, and covered up in that hole: but through the length of time consumed. From three foot deep, we found much wood ashes soft and black as ink, some little bits of an urn, and black and red earth very rotten. Some small lumps of earth red as vermillion: some flints burnt through. Toward the bottom a great quantity of ashes and burnt bones. <margin>Plate XXXI.</margin> From this place I could count 128 barrows in sight. See a vast multiplicity of them, Plate XXXI.

Going from hence more southerly, there is a circular dish-like cavity dug in the chalk, 60 cubits in diameter, like a barrow reversed. It is near a great barrow, the least of the south-western group. <margin>Plate XXXII.</margin> It is between it, and what I call the "bushbarrow," set with thorn-trees, Plate XXXII.

This cavity is seven feet deep in the middle, extremely well turned, and out of it, no doubt, the adjacent barrow is dug. The use of it seems to have been a place for sacrificing and feasting in memory of the dead, as was the ancient custom. It is all overgrown with that pretty shrub *common heather* <note>original Latin: "erica vulgaris"</note>, now in flower, and smelling like honey. We made a large cross-section in its center upon the cardinal points; we found nothing but a bit of red earthen pot.

We dug up one of those I call Druid's barrows, a small tump <note>A local term for a small mound or hillock.</note> enclosed in a large circular ditch. I chose that next to "bushbarrow," westward of it. *Stonehenge* bears hence north-east. We made a cross section ten foot each way,

way, three feet broad over its center, upon the cardinal points. At length we found a squarish hole cut into the solid chalk, in the center of the *tumulus* <note>A burial mound.</note>. It was three feet and a half—namely, two cubits—long, and near two feet broad—namely, one cubit—pointing directly to *Stonehenge*. It was a cubit and a half deep from the surface. This was the <note>original: "domus exilis Plutonia"</note> "meager house of Pluto" <note>A quote from the Roman poet Horace, referring to the cramped nature of the grave.</note> covered with artificial earth, not above a foot thick from the surface. In this little grave we found all the burnt bones of a man, but no signs of an urn. The bank of the circular ditch is on the outside, and is 12 cubits broad. The ditch is 6 cubits broad (the length of a Druid’s staff); the area is 70 cubits in diameter. The whole is 100.

I opened another of these of similar dimensions, next to the one Lord *Pembroke* first opened, south of *Stonehenge*. We found a burnt body in a hole in the chalk, as before. Mr. *Roger Gale* <note>A prominent English antiquary and friend of the author.</note> was with me.

In some other barrows I opened, large burnt bones of horses and dogs were found along with human ones. Also those of other animals, it seemed: of fowl, hares, boars, deer, goats, or the like. And in a great and very flat old-fashioned barrow, west from *Stonehenge*, among such matters, I found bits of red and blue marble, chippings of the stones of the temple. So it is probable the person interred was one of the builders. *Homer* tells us of *Achilles* slaying horses and dogs at the funeral of his friend *Patroclus*.

Lord *Pembroke* told me of a brass sword dug up in a barrow here, which was sent to *Oxford*. In that very old barrow near little *Amesbury*, a very large brass weapon of 20 pounds weight was found, like a pole-axe. It is said to have been given to Colonel *Wyndham*. In the great long barrow farthest north from *Stonehenge*, which I call the north long barrow and suppose to be an Archdruid's, was found one of those brass instruments called <term>celts</term> <note>Prehistoric bronze axe-heads or chisels, which early antiquarians mistakenly thought were used by Druids to cut mistletoe.</note>, which I believe belonged to the Druids, wherewith they cut off the mistletoe, as previously mentioned. Mr. *Stallard* of *Amesbury* gave it to Lord *Burlington*, and it is now in Sir *Hans Sloane*'s cabinet <note>Sir Hans Sloane's massive collection of curiosities became the founding core of the British Museum.</note>: 13 inches long. Someone dug a cell in a barrow east of *Amesbury*, and it was inhabited for some time. There they found all the bones of a horse. This is the sum of what is most material that fell within my observation relating to the barrows about *Stonehenge*. We find evidently that these ancient nations had the custom of burning their dead bodies, probably before the name of *Rome* even existed. Similarly, we read of lachrymatories <note>Small glass bottles found in ancient tombs, once thought to hold the tears of mourners.</note> in scripture, older than *Greek* or *Roman* times, in *Psalm 56:8*.

**Table XXXI.** The barrows in *Lake-field*. This is like a church-yard, the burial-place of some town or large family. I mentioned before that the ditches observable here are the boundaries of parishes, hundreds, or lordships. The countrymen sometimes call this group "the prophets' barrows," because the *French* prophets <note>A group of radical Protestant refugees known as the Camisards who arrived in England in the early 1700s.</note> 30 years ago set up a standard on the largest barrow and preached to the enthusiastic multitude.

**Table XXXIII.** Bush-barrow, a barrow planted with bushes by the shepherds. It is south of *Stonehenge* and commands a pleasant prospect of the temple, the *cursus* <note>A large Neolithic earthwork enclosure resembling a racetrack.</note>, the avenue, and of all the barrows around this plain. You see the hills a little on this side of *Avebury*, whereon runs the *Wansdyke* <note>A long series of ancient defensive earthworks.</note>, the boundary of the *Belgic* kingdom.

**Table XXXIV.** The *tumulus* of *Carvilius* <note>A British king who fought against the Roman invasion.</note> who fought *Julius Caesar*. It is on the other side of *Wilton* (*Carvilium*) by Lord *Pembroke*'s park, and is planted with four trees as one of the vistas to the park.

**Table XXXV.** One of the temples at *Persepolis* <note>An ancient capital of the Persian Empire.</note>, a patriarchal one, open; but made after the style of *Solomon's* temple, square, with mouldings and ornaments. I take it to be of the same age as *Stonehenge*.

The author uses these findings to argue for the antiquity of the site and its builders, comparing them to figures from Homeric and Biblical history.</summary>

### DESCRIBED.

This calls to my memory what the previously mentioned *Dr. Harwood* informed me: he had heard the great *Sir Christopher Wren* <note>The famous architect of St. Paul's Cathedral and a keen observer of antiquities.</note> say that there were structures like *Stonehenge* in *Africa*, which were temples dedicated to *Saturn*. But I need not be tedious in pointing out how absurd the *Monkish* <note>Referring to medieval chroniclers, whom Enlightenment writers often viewed as superstitious or unreliable.</note> reports are; stories of a Christian king building *Stonehenge* as a funeral monument for the *British* nobility who were slaughtered in the monastery of *Ambresbury* <note>modern Amesbury</note>. At the same time, these accounts say their bodies were buried in the churchyard of that very monastery. Nor should I dwell on how they confuse the names of *Ambrosius* the king, *Ambrius* the abbot, the town, the abbey, and the mountain of *Ambry*, and perhaps even *Merlin* as well, for one of them was called *Ambrosius*. Yet their claim—that the building came out of *Africa* into *Spain*, then into *Ireland*, and finally into *Britain*, and that it was built here in the same form through magical arts and that the stones have medicinal virtues—these ideas lead us toward the original truth: that the *Druid* <note>Ancient Celtic priests, scholars, and judges.</note> founders were responsible, and that *Stonehenge* originally had the name *Ambres*, from which the nearby town of *Ambresbury* took its name.

To pursue this matter a little further: between *Stonehenge* and the town, overlooking the river on high ground, is a beautiful and ancient camp. It is commonly called *Vespasian's* <note>Named for the Roman Emperor Vespasian, who campaigned in Britain as a general.</note> and is quite likely attributed to him. We have often had occasion to mention it before. That great man—destined by Providence to carry out ultimate vengeance against the *Jews* and thus fulfill our Savior’s predictions—paved his way to the imperial throne through his successes in this place. Having conquered the Isle of *Wight*, he followed his good fortune further up into this region, where he established this camp and another across the heath called *Yanesbury*, which seems to keep the latter part of his name. The camp we are speaking of near *Ambresbury* is a rectangle, perfectly positioned on a bend of the river that protects one side and one end. Inside it sits an old burial mound <term>barrow: an ancient burial mound</term> which undoubtedly belonged to this plain and the temple of *Stonehenge* before the camp was even built. It is fascinating to notice that the road from *Stonehenge* to *Ambresbury* follows the path of the camp's main street <note>original: "via praetoria"</note>. The general’s headquarters <note>original: "praetorium"</note> was in the section south of the road, between the road and the river toward Little *Ambresbury*. There is another gate at the lower end to the north, known in the *Roman* language as the standard main gate <note>original: "porta praetoria ordinaria"</note>. I now believe that *Stonehenge* was originally called the *Ambres*; the camp was then named *Ambresburgh*, and the town below took its name from that.

Mr. *Camden* <note>William Camden (1551–1623), a pioneering English historian and antiquarian.</note> writes, "that near *Penzance* in *Cornwall*, there is a very remarkable stone called the *main Ambre*. Although it is of enormous size, you can move it with a single finger, yet a great number of men cannot shift it from its spot." There is an illustration of it in *Norden's* history of *Cornwall*, page 48. I have seen one of these "rocking stones," as they are commonly called, in *Derbyshire*. Mr. *Toland*, in his history of the Druids, also mentions them and says they exist in *Wales* and *Ireland*. Sir *Robert Sibbald* mentions them in *Scotland*; all are correctly judged to have been the work of the Druids. Sir *Robert*, speaking of the rocking stone near *Balvaird* (or the *Bard's Town*) in *Fife*, says: "I am informed that this stone was broken by the soldiers of the usurper *Cromwell*. It was then discovered that its movement was caused by a protruding 'yolk' in the middle of the bottom surface of the top stone, which fit into a hollow in the surface of the bottom stone." This is the same mechanical trick used for the stones at *Stonehenge*, but applied here by the Druids for movement, whereas at *Stonehenge* it was used for stability. I call these joints mortise and tenon <term>mortise and tenon: a joint made by inserting a projection (tenon) into a hole (mortise)</term>. I previously noted they were egg-shaped, which Sir *Robert* calls a "yolk." The *Main Amber* in *Cornwall* was also destroyed during the civil wars by one of *Oliver Cromwell's* governors. These "reformers" believed these works were objects of superstition. *Main Ambre* is the Ambrosial stone <note>original: "lapis Ambrosius, or petra Ambrosia"</note>. And that name leads us to consider—

<summary>The author connects the name Stonehenge to "Ambres," arguing it was founded by Druids and links it to nearby Amesbury and Vespasian's Roman camp. He also discusses "rocking stones" like the Main Ambre in Cornwall, identifying them as Druidic engineering similar to Stonehenge's joints.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author’s argument that ancient stone circles like Stonehenge are rooted in the patriarchal traditions of the Old Testament, specifically the "open temples" and "anointed stones" of figures like Jacob and Moses.</meta>

# STONEHENGE

> “...for a pillar; and poured oil upon the top of it, and called the place
> “ *Beth-el*, that is, the house of God. Then he vowed, that if God would
> “ please to prosper him in his journey, and bring him back into his own
> “ country, he would build a temple there, and consecrate to God the tithe
> “ of his substance, as was the manner in those times.”

This is in reality a votive, <note>votive: given or consecrated in fulfillment of a vow</note> patriarchal temple, altar, or house of God, which Jacob not only vows to build, but at the same time endows. The stone which *Jacob* anointed was not an altar in the strict sense, lying flat on the ground for making a drink offering, but he set it up as a pillar. It was one of the upright stones, which the scripture calls pillars because they stand by themselves; part of a circle of stones enclosing the altar. By the act of anointing it, *Jacob* consecrated it—as was the custom then—dedicating it to a sacred purpose as a promise that he would fulfill his vow in due time. And he did indeed fulfill this, as seen in *Genesis* chapter 35, by building the famous temple of *Bethel*. Later, *Jeroboam* set up one of his golden calves there, and it was eventually destroyed by the Roman Emperor *Vespasian*.

> In *Exodus* 24:4, “we have an instance of *Moses* rising up early in the
> “ morning and building an altar, and setting up 12 pillars around it.”

This occurred before the Tabernacle was constructed, which introduced the custom of covered temples.

But so famous was that patriarchal temple of *Jacob’s* at *Bethel*, that the heathens called all their temples of that sort—when they were corrupted for idol-worship—*Baetylia* <note>original: "Bathylia", meaning sacred stones believed to be divine or endowed with life</note>, *Baetyl stones* <note>original: "lapides Bætyli"</note>, and the like. This is actually just another way of saying *Ambrosial stone* <note>original: "lapis Ambrosius"</note>, or our own *Main Ambre* <term>Main Ambre: A traditional name for "rocking stones"—large natural boulders balanced so they rock at a touch, often associated with ancient rituals</term>. According to custom, the legendary *Greeks*, having lost the true history of where these stones came from, attached many strange stories to them; such as the myth of *Saturn* devouring a stone wrapped in a skin instead of his son *Jupiter*. This story seems to be a distorted memory of people praying at these stone temples in the name of a mediating deity, just as the patriarchs did. Furthermore, the historian *Sanchoniathon* tells us that the god *Ouranus* <note>original: "Ouranus", the Greek personification of the sky</note> devised *Baetylia*, or "animated stones." By this, he means our rocking stones, also known as *gygonian* stones. I shall show in my discourse on that subject that by *Ouranus*, he actually means the righteous *Noah*, who, following patriarchal custom, "builded an altar unto *Jehovah*" (*Genesis* 8:20), meaning one of these stone temples. In time, through the corruption of humanity, these places were desecrated for the use of idols. Because of this, when God established the Mosaic Law, He was obliged to forbid the use of these open temples and introduce the covered Tabernacle to oppose heathenism and for other important reasons.

We now understand the meaning of "anointed stones" in antiquity, and why an olive tree is shown next to stones on *Tyrian* <note>Tyrian: relating to the ancient Phoenician city of Tyre</note> coins. As the very learned author of *Greek Antiquities* <note>original: "Archaeologia Græca"</note> observes regarding consecration, “they were more or less sumptuous and expensive, like other parts of divine worship, according to the ability of the worshippers.” The young *Jacob*, being a traveler, used plain oil from his travel provisions <note>original: "viaticum"</note>; others used perfumed oil, or *ambrosia*. That author quotes from *Athenaeus* the method of consecrating the statue of *Jupiter Ctesias* <note>Jupiter Ctesias: a Greek representation of Zeus as a protector of property</note> with a liquid offering called *ambrosia*; others used oil, prayers, and drink offerings (*Exodus* 30:22). We also see the holy, precious ointment created under the *Jewish* law for a similar purpose. Indeed, we still use such oil for the coronation of our own kings to this day.

The *Tyrian Hercules*, who built the city of *Tyre* and featured these "Ambrosial stones" <note>original: "petræ Ambroſiæ"</note> on those coins, lived (if I am not mistaken) as early as the time when *Jacob* was anointing the stone at *Bethel*. The great scholar *Bochart*, who looked deeply into *Phoenician* learning, considers it a settled matter that in the time of *Joshua*, the *Phoenicians* sent many colonies to the Mediterranean coasts and even as far as the Atlantic Ocean. In the preface to his excellent work *Canaan*, he says he strongly suspects that colonies traveled abroad this way even before that time. Specifically, he asserts that *Hercules*, mentioned in the writings of *Eusebius*—

## CHAPTER XII.

*A conjecture about the time of the founding of Stonehenge. An uniform variation in setting these works, not to be accounted for, but by supposing the Druids used a magnetical compass. Their leader, the Tyrian Hercules, was possessed of a compass-box. The oracle of Jupiter Ammon had a compass-box. The golden fleece at Colchis was a compass-box. Both these temples were founded by Apher, Hercules's companion, and grandson to Abraham. Apher, Aphricus, or Phryxus the same person, seems to have given name to Britain. The Druids set their temples and other works by it. The history of the mariner's compass, since that time. The history of the variation of the magnetic needle. A conjecture of the time of building Stonehenge from thence.*

IN my enquiries into these works of the ancient <term>Druids</term> <note>The priestly class of the ancient Celts, whom the author credits with advanced scientific knowledge.</note> in our island, I observed a greater exactness in placing them, with regard to the quarters of the heavens, than one would expect in works seemingly so rude; and in so remote an age, to which we must necessarily refer them. What more particularly moved my attention, was a certain variation from cardinal points, which I observed regular and uniform in the works of one place, and that variation was different, in works of another place; yet equally regular and uniform in that place. Suppose (for instance) the works about <term>Abury</term> <note>Now known as Avebury, a massive Neolithic henge monument in Wiltshire, England.</note> in <term>Wiltshire</term> generally vary 9 or 10 degrees to the left hand, from cardinal points; *that is*, westward from the north. And the works at <term>Stonehenge</term> generally vary to the right hand, from cardinal points, and that to the amount of 6 or 7 degrees.

The principal diameter or ground-line of <term>Stonehenge</term>, leading from the entrance, up the middle of the temple, to the high altar, (from which line the whole work is formed) varies about that amount southward of the north-east point. The intent of the founders of <term>Stonehenge</term>, was to set the entrance full north-east, being the point where the sun rises, or nearly, at the <term>summer solstice</term> <note>The longest day of the year, usually June 21st, when the sun reaches its highest point in the sky.</note>. As well because that is the farthest elongation of the great celestial luminary, northward; the complement of our earthly felicity, in ripening the fruits of the earth: as because then they celebrated one of their principal religious meetings or festivals, with sacrifices, public games, and the like. Such was the custom of all the ancient nations. The <term>Isthmian, Nemean, Olympian, and Pythian</term> games, famous in the works of the learned nations: those of <term>Tyre</term> <note>An ancient Phoenician port city in modern-day Lebanon.</note> mentioned in <term>II Maccabees</term> iv. 18. <note>A book of the biblical Apocrypha; the passage refers to games held every five years in Tyre.</note> dedicated to their and our founder, the ancient <term>Tyrian Hercules</term> <note>Specifically the god Melqart, whom the author believes was a historical figure who led a Phoenician colony to Britain.</note>, who, I suppose, conducted the first <term>Phoenician</term> colony, with our <term>Druids</term>, into <term>Britain</term>: these were all held at this time of the year. A custom continued from patriarchal times.

This exactness with which the <term>Druids</term> set their works, and the uniformity of their variation, make me believe, this variation was not the effect of chance or negligence.

By a superficial reflection upon it, we should be apt to suspect, it was owing to their observing the sun’s rising on the longest day of the year, or <term>summer solstice</term>, and setting their line by it. For this is supposed to be a method by which they formerly set our <term>Churches</term>: marking the sun’s rising at the <term>equinox</term> <note>The two times of the year when day and night are of equal length.</note>. But the <term>Druids</term> were too good <term>astronomers</term> and <term>mathematicians</term> to need so mean an artifice: nor does it correspond to the quan-

He links the Druids to Phoenician colonies led by a historical Hercules, a grandson of Abraham.</summary>

precisely enough. Besides, this same variation appears where it cannot possibly regard the sun's rising at that time.

For, I observed the like variation, or very near, in all the other parts relating to this temple before taken notice of; beside the avenue leading up to the temple from the north east, in a straight line; which has the before-mentioned variation all the way. At the bottom of the hill, this avenue divides into two wings, each going off from the last mentioned part, with a decent sweep; the one to the left hand, westward, the other to the right hand, eastward. They go off with a like angle, and that angle varies the like quantity. The western wing goes to the <term>cursus</term> <note>A long, narrow earthwork enclosure, likely used for ancient ceremonies or races.</note>, before observed, the place upon the downs, half a mile off <term>Stonehenge</term>, made for races with chariots and horses. The right hand wing of the avenue runs directly eastward for a mile together, pointing to a place on an angle of the river, called <term>Radfin</term>. This part of the avenue, which was intended by the founders, to have been drawn precisely east and west, varies about 5 or 6 degrees to the south.

Likewise, that great work of the <term>cursus</term> itself, which stretches its length across the downs, from east to west, like a line of latitude upon the globe, varies such a like quantity, from true east and west, the same way. The <term>meridian line</term> <note>An imaginary north-south line used for astronomical and geographic alignment.</note> of <term>Stonehenge</term> passes exactly through the middle of this <term>cursus</term>. Further, at the east end of this <term>cursus</term>, the huge bank of earth, above 200 foot long, made across the end of the <term>cursus</term>, as a *turning point* <note>original: "meta"; used in ancient racing to mark the point where chariots turned.</note>, and whereon sat the princes and judges of the prizes: This bank of earth is drawn exactly at a right angle with the <term>cursus</term>, consequently due north and south, but with the variation before spoken of. These, and other like observations here, as well as in other <term>Druid Works</term>, appeared to me no otherwise to be accounted for, but that the Druids used a <term>magnetical compass</term>, in laying down the works: and that the needle varied so much, at that time, from the true meridian line.

I remember I opened this affair, near 20 years ago, to <term>Dr. Halley</term> <note>Edmond Halley (1656–1742), the famous astronomer who studied magnetic variation.</note>, who was of the same sentiment. Nor am I the first who suspected the <term>Phoenicians</term> of old were possessed of this great secret, as well as the <term>Chinese</term>, from times immemorial. I am not moved to think otherwise by what <term>Bochart</term> writes against it. The very name of the <term>magnet</term> *the stone of Hercules* <note>original: "lapis Heraclius"</note> strongly suggests, the <term>Tyrian</term> navigator before-mentioned knew it, as is well argued by <term>Fuller</term> in his *Miscellanies*, IV. 19. And many things occur in the mythology of the ancients, wherein (if I mistake not) I discern most evident traces of this knowledge of the directive power of the <term>magnet</term>. We are not to despise the fables of the ancients, but to make the best use of them, and search out for their latent truths. My predecessor <term>Cumberland</term>, observes in *<term>Sanchoniathon</term>*, p. 325.
> “that *<term>Apollodorus</term>* (for instance) hath many truths in his mythic history, derived from the tradition of <term>Phoenicians</term> and <term>Egyptians</term>, planting <term>Athens</term>.”

And the <term>Greeks</term>, those happy practitioners in writing, as well as other arts, took the unlucky turn of the marvellous, to so exorbitant a degree, as to write nothing without it. In <term>Apollodorus</term>, put out by the learned <term>Dr. Gale</term>, p. 114. we have an account of the 10th labour of <term>Hercules</term>, his conquest of <term>Cadiz</term>, or <term>Erythea</term>. We are told, the hero set up the 2 pillars at the Straits' mouth, at <term>Gibraltar</term>, or then <term>Tartessus</term>: which we may reasonably suppose some temple made of these rough stones, or some main *sacred stones* <note>original: "ambres"</note>, like those we mentioned before, the *ambrosial stones* <note>original: "petræ ambrosiæ"</note> in the <term>Tyrian</term> coast. “Then,” says our author, “going on his journey, the rays of the <term>sun</term> were so vehement upon him, that he had the boldness to draw his bow against him. <header>STONEHENGE</header>

History, quoted both in *Athenaeus* <note>A Greek rhetorician and grammarian, known for his work *The Deipnosophists*.</note> and *Macrobius* <note>A Roman provincial who wrote the *Saturnalia*, a collection of ancient lore.</note>, tells a story somewhat like that of *Apollodorus*, but more particular. *Servius* in his commentary on the *Aeneid*, Book VII, mentions it, but as some of the former authors did, he makes the cup of brass instead of gold. *Alexander of Ephesus* says the like. All these are very ancient writers. *Lucian* <note>A Syrian-Greek satirist.</note> says that *Hercules* sailed in a sea-conch shell. What can we understand by all this, mentioned by so many grave authors, but a <term>compass box</term> <note>The author is speculating that the mythological "golden cup" given to Hercules by the Sun was actually an early magnetic compass, allowing him to navigate the open ocean.</note>, which enabled him to sail the great ocean and penetrate to our northern island, being less exposed to the sun’s vehement heat? Add to this, in the same place, *Apollodorus* speaks of his fighting *Albion* and *Dercynus* (called *Bergion* by *Mela*), sons of *Neptune*; these were the most ancient names of the *Britannic Isles* before they were called *Britain*. *Diodorus Siculus*, in his fourth book, delivers a similar account of this tenth labour of *Hercules*, but in a purely historical manner. He adds that when he returned by way of *Sicily*, he dedicated a grove to the hero *Geryon*, where, up to his own time, the people performed religious rites. For this practice of sacred groves, we know our *Druids* <note>The high-ranking priestly class in ancient Celtic societies.</note> were famous. He built a temple likewise at *Gades* <note>Modern Cádiz, Spain.</note>. We are not to suppose it a covered edifice, like what posterity called a temple, but an open one, according to the mode of those days. Covered temples, at that time, were a thing unknown in the world. Afterward, a magnificent temple, properly speaking, was built there to him. *Mela* witnesses that it was our *Egyptian Hercules* who was worshipped there. For I suppose our *Egyptian* and the *Tyrian Hercules* to be one and the same. The same is mentioned by the name of *Assis* in *Manetho’s* <note>An Egyptian priest and historian.</note> 17th Dynasty, and in the works of *Josephus* (in his book *Against Apion*), *Africanus*, *Eusebius*, and *Syncellus*. *Apollonius* (Book II, 14) writes that it was not the *Theban* but the *Egyptian Hercules* that came to *Gades*, which is confirmed by *Hecateus*. And *Herodotus*, in *Euterpe* <note>The second book of Herodotus’s *Histories*.</note>, says *Hercules* is a very ancient deity among the *Egyptians*, but not so among the *Greeks*. And I suppose this hero lived at, or very near, the time of the patriarch *Abraham*.

These were the times about the beginning of idolatry. And *Hercules* was far from being an idolater himself, though worshipped afterwards for his great exploits, and perhaps on this very account of his inventing or knowing the use of the compass. This is the *Hercules* kneeling on one knee, a constellation in heaven, noted by *Dionysius of Halicarnassus*, *Tzetzes*, *Hyginus*, *Aeschylus*, and others. It seems to indicate his piety, for which the astronomers who were his disciples placed him in the heavens. He kneels upon the arctic circle and supports the zodiac on his shoulders; though this is not understood by the painting on our modern globes. The *Phoenicians*, his successors in the tin trade of *Britain*, kept the trade and the very name of the island as a great secret, as well as the use of the compass, until it was lost with them. But it seems highly probable, because *Lucian* describes *Hercules* with a sphere in his hand, that he established the present <term>asterisms</term> <note>Recognizable patterns of stars, smaller than official constellations.</note> of the zodiac, and his successors, the *Phoenicians*, propagated them.

It is next to our present purpose to consider that famous oracle of *Jupiter Ammon* in *Africa*, to be referred to the most early times of idolatry; rendered illustrious by *Alexander the Great* taking a journey to it. This gives us the opportunity of knowing something of it.

-> Although for the Ethiopian peoples, and the blessed tribes of the Arabs, <-
-> and the Indians, Jupiter Ammon is the only one. <note>Original Latin: *Quamvis Æthiopum populis, Arabumq; beatis / Gentibus, ac Indis, unus sit Jupiter Ammon.*</note> Lucan. <-

All these nations, along with *Egypt* and *Africa*, were peopled chiefly by the posterity of *Ham*. They were the first that fell into idolatry and worshipped their common progenitor, called *Amynus* in *Sanchoniathon*. *Hecateus* says *Amun*, as the *Egyptians* write it, is the word used by those who invoke God, and that they meant something very mysterious by it. The history of its origin is this: *Bacchus*, the hero or demigod, traveling through the sandy deserts of *Africa* with a great army, was perishing with thirst; he prayed to his father *Jupiter* for relief, who sent a ram that showed him a spring, saving him and his host. Out of gratitude, the hero builds a temple there to the deity who thus aided him under the form of a ram. There is no room to doubt,

There is no doubt that this is in part copied from the history of the children of **Israel** in the **Arabian wilderness**. They have added to it a name and a concept borrowed from patriarchal tradition: a divine person symbolized by a ram—horned and anointed, which are one and the same. We Christians mean the **Messiah**. Innumerable passages in ancient authors, which I might cite, and countless monuments of antiquity in sculpture, show that **Jupiter Ammon** was depicted as a ram, or with a ram’s head and horns. They applied the patriarchal notion of the **Messiah** to their ancestor **Ham** <note>The son of Noah; the author suggests the Egyptians deified their own progenitor by conflating him with biblical prophecy.</note> in an idolatrous way, deifying him under that character. There is a very remarkable passage in **Herodotus** <note>The famous Greek historian of the 5th century BCE.</note> which is worth our while to transcribe.

In *Euterpe* <note>The title of the second book of Herodotus's *Histories*.</note> chapter 42, that author tells us why the **Theban Egyptians** hold the sheep in such high regard:
> "**Hercules**, after his persistent pleading to **Jupiter** that he might have the honor of seeing him in person, at length prevailed. And the god consented to reveal himself to his view under this device: **Jupiter** cut off a ram's head, put the skin over his own head, and thus appeared to **Hercules**. For this reason, the **Egyptians** made the statue of **Jupiter** with a ram's head, and they call **Jupiter Ammoun**. Because of this, they hold sheep as sacred animals and never kill them but once a year, upon the festival day of **Jupiter**, when only one ram is sacrificed and its head placed upon the statue of **Jupiter**; all who are present beat the ram, and at last it is buried in a sacred urn."

It is impossible not to see that this is derived from the history recorded in **Exodus 33**. **Moses** repeatedly asks **Jehovah** that he might see Him; he calls it "seeing His glory." He is eventually answered:
> "I will make all my goodness pass before you, and I will proclaim the name of **Jehovah** before you. You cannot see my face, but I will put you in a cleft of the rock and cover you with my hand while I pass by. You shall see only my back."

Here, He clearly promises **Moses** that he shall see Him in a symbolic form. In the next chapter, **Jehovah** descended in the luminous cloud, or **Shechinah** <term>Shechinah: A Hebrew term for the visible manifestation of God's presence, typically described as a divine light or cloud.</term>, and proclaimed the **name of Jehovah**. He recites those attributes that relate to His dealings with mankind in the clearest possible light:
> "His goodness and mercy, and patience, forgiving iniquity, transgression, and sin; but adds, he will by no means clear the guilty, but visit the fathers' iniquity upon the children."

In this, our original and fatal transgression <note>A reference to Original Sin.</note> is sufficiently hinted at, showing that God's justice is equal to His mercy, and the necessity of a divine redemption through sacrifice—which in scriptural language is called "the lamb slain from the foundation of the world."

All the most ancient nations had knowledge of this from patriarchal tradition. When they fell into idolatry, they applied these good concepts to their new false religions and made statues based on the symbolic and figurative forms of speech used in the true religion. Their sacrifice of the ram on the festival day of **Jupiter**, their beating of the ram, placing its head on the statue of their deity, and burying it in a sacred urn—all most evidently point to the notions they had in the earliest times of the suffering state of the **Messiah**. Such, in short, was the origin of **Jupiter Ammon**. However, it appears from what learned authors have written regarding **Curtius's** <note>Quintus Curtius Rufus, a Roman historian.</note> description of his statue, that a magnetic compass box made up one considerable part of his sacred objects. We read this in **Hyde's** *The Religion of the Ancient Persians* (page 495) <note>original Latin title: *Historia Religionis Veterum Persarum*</note>, in **Curtius** as published by **Pitiscus**, and by the Jesuit **Rader**, and **Schottus** in **Ortelius**, by **Fuller**, **Herwart**, and others.
> "This compass box, along with the statue of the deity, was set in a golden ship (the golden cup of **Hercules**) and carried in procession on the shoulders of the priests, accompanied by women singing a hymn in their own language."

I do not doubt that the circumstance of carrying this golden ship on the shoulders of the priests is an imitation of the **Mosaic Ark** in the march of the **Israelites** through the wilderness during their forty years' pilgrimage. Though they misunderstood the reason for it, the **Jewish**...

### D E S C R I B E D.

There is no doubt that this is in part copied from the history of the children of **Israel** in the **Arabian** wilderness. They have added to it a name and concept borrowed from patriarchal tradition, concerning a divine person symbolized by a ram; both horned and anointed, which signify the same thing. We Christians understand this to be the **Messiah**. Innumerable passages in ancient authors, which I might cite, and countless monuments of antiquity in sculpture, show that **Jupiter Ammon** was represented as a ram, with a ram's head and ram's horns. They applied the patriarchal concept of the **Messiah** to their ancestor **Ham** in an idolatrous way, and deified him under that character. There is a very remarkable passage in **Herodotus** <note>A Greek historian from the 5th century BC, often called the "Father of History."</note> which is worth our while to transcribe.

In **Euterpe** <note>original: "Euterpe," the second book of Herodotus’s *Histories*</note> chapter 42, that author tells us why the **Theban Egyptians** pay such great regard to the sheep.

> "Hercules, upon his persistence to **Jupiter** that he might have the honor of seeing him personally, at length prevailed. And the god consented to show himself to his view under this device: namely, **Jupiter** cut off a ram's head, put the skin over his own head, and thus appeared to **Hercules**. This is why the Egyptians made the statue of **Jupiter** with a ram's head and call him **Jupiter Ammon**. For this reason, they hold sheep as sacred animals and never kill them except once a year, on the festival day of **Jupiter**, when only one ram is sacrificed and its head placed upon the statue of **Jupiter**; all who are present then beat the ram, and at last, it is buried in a sacred urn."

It is impossible not to see that this is derived from the history recorded in **Exodus 33**. **Moses** repeatedly desires of **Jehovah** <note>The Hebrew name for God</note> that he might see Him. He calls it "seeing His glory." He is answered at length:

> "I will make all my goodness pass before thee, and I will proclaim the name of **Jehovah** before thee. Thou canst not see my face, but I will put thee in a cleft of the rock, and cover thee with my hand while I pass by. Thou shalt see my back parts only."

> "His goodness and mercy, and long-suffering, forgiving iniquity, transgression, and sin; but adds, He will by no means clear the guilty, but visit the fathers' iniquity upon the children."

In this, our original and fatal transgression <note>A reference to the Fall of Man in Eden</note> is sufficiently suggested, along with the fact that God's justice is equal to His mercy, and the necessity of a divine redemption by sacrifice, which in scriptural language is called "the lamb slain from the foundation of the world."

All this the most ancient nations had knowledge of through patriarchal tradition. When they fell into idolatry, they applied these righteous concepts to their new false religions and created statues based on the symbolic and figurative forms of speech used in the true religion. Their sacrifice of the ram on the festival day of **Jupiter**, the beating of the ram, the placing of its head on the statue of their deity, and its burial in a sacred urn—all these most evidently point to the ideas they held in the earliest times regarding the suffering state of the **Messiah**. Such was the origin, in short, of **Jupiter Ammon**.

> "This compass box, along with the statue of the deity, was set in a golden ship (the golden cup of **Hercules**) and carried in procession on the shoulders of the priests, accompanied by women singing a hymn in their own language."

I do not doubt that the practice of carrying this golden ship on the shoulders of the priests is an imitation of the **Mosaic ark** <term>The Ark of the Covenant, the sacred chest containing the stone tablets of the Law</term> used during the march of the **Israelites** through the wilderness during their forty years' pilgrimage. <meta>Continuing from page 60. The author continues an argument that ancient myths were actually misunderstood descriptions of early technology, specifically the magnetic compass.</meta>

## DESCRIBED.

God's holy spirit had deserted them, their writers became the greatest makers of fables in the world, and, if possible, out-did the *Greeks* in that regard.

One would imagine the fashion of these most ancient charts was to divide the circle into 12 parts and affix the celestial signs of the zodiac to them, beginning with the east at <term>Aries<note>The Ram, the first sign of the zodiac</note></term>, where the sun rises at the equinoxes. From this, they might call the compass box by the name of *Aries*, as it showed the east where *Aries* is placed. Similarly, the *Turks* and *Arabians* now call it the <term>direction-finder<note>original: "kibla noma"; a device used to find the Qibla, or the direction of Mecca</note></term>, showing the *Qibla*, or south point, the direction toward which they turn their faces in devotion. In the same way, we only look for the north point and call it the <term>lode-stone<note>a naturally magnetized piece of the mineral magnetite</note></term>, because it shows the "lode-star" or North Pole. But it is all the same; once any one point in a circle is found, the rest are found too.

From what has been said, it seems probable that the fable of the hero sent from *Jupiter* into the sandy deserts of *Africa*, guided by a ram, is actually a story of traveling over those immense plains with the help of a compass, which they called by the name of "a ram" or "a golden ram." The possessors of the ancient <term>oracle of Ammon<note>A famous oracle in the Siwa Oasis dedicated to the god Amun, who was often depicted with the horns of a ram</note></term> had such a secret, which they cunningly applied to the sacred rites of their deity. Probably in that very early age, they had not improved its use to the level we enjoy today, using a needle set upon a central pin. Instead, having discovered the <term>verticity<note>the property of a magnet to point toward the magnetic poles</note></term> of the magnet, they put it into a small boat intended to float on water, where it would be free to turn itself toward its proper direction. This is the opinion of the learned Dr. *Wallis* in the *Philosophical Transactions*, number 278. This "boat" gave the myth-makers a reason to call *Hercules*'s vessel a "golden cup," because cups were made in the shape of a boat and shared the same name, <term>boat-cup<note>original Latin: "cymbium"</note></term>.

Those learned commentators on <term>Curtius<note>likely Quintus Curtius Rufus, a Roman historian who wrote about Alexander the Great</note></term> mentioned above agree there was a stone carried along with the statue of *Ammon* in a golden ship. Perhaps ancient navigators took the hint from this, applying the figure of the ram to their compasses, however they were shaped, and gave it the name of "the ram" or "the golden fleece," which the *Greek* fables—their most ancient history—are full of. From this, their ships derived an "oracular" quality. *Phrixus*'s ship, the "golden ram," was said to speak when needed; this, along with the amazing properties of the magnetic stone, would easily persuade even those who were not common folk that there was a divine, metaphysical, and therefore oracular principle within it. 

In the early days of idolatry, the "evil agent" <note>a reference to the Devil</note> who was watchful to pervert everything for his own purposes, would not fail to make great use of the secrets of the magnet. The entire concept of oracles among the pagans was caused by the devil's mockery of God's dealings with the patriarchs and the *Jews*. However, I believe the *Egyptians* took their custom of carrying a boat in all their religious processions from this magnetic boat, as both *Herodotus* and *Plutarch* inform us. They intended it to signify the movement and descent of divine ideas from the supreme mind—especially the very source and principle of those ideas—and it must be admitted that the symbol was chosen. For this reason, the top and bottom of the border of the celebrated <term>Table of Isis<note>original: "Isiac table" or "Bembin table"; a famous bronze tablet with Egyptian-style engravings, once thought to contain ancient wisdom</note></term> are adorned with a boat. In one is a ram, in the other a bull, meaning the origin of the chain of ideas flowing from the divine mind. It is highly probable that the ram is a copy of *Jupiter Ammon*'s boat mentioned by *Curtius*. I suppose this is *Herwart*'s opinion, though I have not yet seen his book. I shall discuss this at greater length in my explanation of the *Bembine Table*. Nevertheless, *Herodotus* tells us in his second book that the temple of *Jupiter Ammon* originated in *Phoenicia*. I only mention this for the sake of those who overstate the antiquity of *Egypt*.

We learn in *Plutarch*'s discourse <term>*On Isis and Osiris*<note>original Latin title: "de Isid. & Osir."</note></term> that the ship *Argos* of the *Greeks* was, in reality, the ship that our *Hercules* sailed around the world in.

<summary>This page argues that ancient myths, such as the Golden Fleece and the Oracle of Ammon, were actually based on early versions of the magnetic compass. The author suggests that the "ram" and "golden boat" symbols were used by ancients to hide the secret of magnetic navigation.</summary>

<meta>Continuing from page 61. The author is elaborating on his theory that the "Golden Fleece" and other mythical objects were actually ancient magnetic compasses used by descendants of Abraham to navigate deserts and seas.</meta>

# STONEHENGE

Further, this prophetic ship is named *Argos*, as my friend *Mr. Baxter* states in his *Glossary of Roman Antiquities* <note>original: "gloss. ant. rom."</note>, deriving from the *Hebrew* and *Syrian* word *argan*, meaning an ark. This confirms what I said above regarding the carrying of the ship of *Ammon* on the shoulders of the priests. *Strabo*, in the second book of his *Geography*, mentions the temple of *Leucothea* <note>A sea goddess in Greek mythology</note>, built by *Phrixus* at *Colchis*; he notes there was an oracle there, and that a sheep was never sacrificed at that place. This shows its relationship to the temple of *Jupiter Ammon* <note>An oracle in the Libyan desert where the god was symbolized by a ram</note>. *Leucothea* is the name of consecration given to the mother of *Hercules*, while *Hercules* himself was called *Palaemon*; both were made sea deities, stemming from the extraordinary fame of *Hercules* as the first and greatest sea captain. *Pausanias*, in his *Description of Greece* <note>original: "in Atticis"</note>, says he was buried in the *Corinthian Isthmus*, where the *Isthmian* games were held in his memory. But *Mela* writes that his remains were at *Gades* <note>Modern-day Cádiz, Spain</note>. It is probable there was only an honorary monument to him at the *Isthmus* as its founder, similar to the honorary monument of *Iolaus* mentioned as being among the *Thebans*, near the *Stadium*, on page 42.

*Mr. Baxter*, in his *Glossary of Roman Antiquities* under the entry for *Ascania*, considers *Phrixus* to be *Aphricus*, the same person as *Jupiter Ammon*, or at least the founder of his temple—or rather, the temple that existed prior to *Jupiter Ammon*. We should not pay much mind to the clever tricks of the *Greeks*, who pull all celebrated events and people of antiquity into the history of their own country. *Aphricus*, no doubt, is the *Aphre* mentioned before, the son of *Midian* and grandson of *Abraham*; whom *Cleodemus* describes as an associate of *Hercules* in his *Libyan* wars. *Josephus* identifies him as the conqueror of *Libya* and says that he gave his name to *Africa*. It is not unlikely that he is the hero who traveled over these barren sands with the help of the compass, just as his countrymen the *Arabians* have practiced from time immemorial when traveling over their own deserts. He likely erected a <term>patriarchal temple<note>A place of worship following the traditions of the biblical patriarchs like Abraham, before they devolved into polytheism</note></term> there; in the time of his descendants, it degenerated into the idolatrous temple of *Jupiter Ammon*. There, the compass box of the hero remained and was converted into one of the pagan sacred objects.

It is no strange matter if they at another time call this same hero *Bacchus*, thereby confusing him with the similar travels of the *Israelites* through the *Arabian* deserts. We should not expect these ancient histories, which are wrapped in fables, to be absolutely consistent. But if this account is close to the truth, then we may imagine the same *Aphre* (called *Phrixus* by the *Greeks*, according to *Mr. Baxter*) crossed the *Hellespont*, led the expedition into *Colchis*, and built a similar temple there. A compass box, called the "golden ram," was likewise made an object of their adoration. This is strongly confirmed by the reports of *Herodotus* and *Diodorus Siculus*, who say the *Colchians* practiced the rite of circumcision—a fact that scholars cannot otherwise explain, but which appears plain here: these people were the descendants of *Abraham*. They say, at the same time, that the *Ethiopians* practice the same; and that it is no recent custom among them, but has existed from the beginning. I believe by *Ethiopians* they mean *Arabians*, who are people descended from *Abraham*. *Herodotus* likewise says the *Egyptians* circumcised, which must be explained in this same way; some *Arabian* or *Ethiopian* nation brought the custom to them. As further confirmation of *Phrixus* being *Aphricus*, *Bochart* shows that the *Colchian* and *Hebrew* languages are closely related. Thus we may account for what *Mr. Toland* says on page 133: that the idiom of the *Irish* language (which we suppose to be a remnant of the most ancient Oriental tongue) has a mixture of *Arabic* in it.

I saw a book in *Dr. Mead's* library, *Museum of Unknown Spanish Medals* <note>original: "Museo de las medallas deſconocidas Espanolas"</note>. On page 35, numbers 82 and 83 are two ancient unknown medals, such as are often found in *Spain*. The first shows a head on the front—not of the best craftsmanship, but heroic enough—wearing a necklace. Behind it is ATHRA <note>original: "ΑΘΡΑ"</note> in the old *Phoenician* character, which resembles the *Samaritan*. On the back is a horseman, and at the bottom <note>original: "exergue"</note> another word in similar <term>Punic<note>The language or script of ancient Carthage, derived from Phoenician</note></term> characters. The other, number 83, has the same head on the front, but without the necklace:

and **AFRA** <note>original: ΑΦΡΑ</note> before, in plain *Greek*, behind a <term>dolphin</term>. The reverse is the same as the last. There is another such <term>coin</term> in the same book, with no difference except that the name and <term>dolphin</term> are transposed. I truly believe this is our <term>Aphra</term>, or <term>Apher</term> (called <term>Epher</term> in our *English* translation of Genesis 25:4), struck by some city in <term>Spain</term> that acknowledged him as their founder.

It is remarkable enough what Mr. <term>Norden</term> <note>John Norden (1547–1625), an English topographer</note> writes in his history of <term>Cornwall</term>. The *Cornish* men universally suppose that the *Jews* are the people who first worked in their <term>rocks</term> for <term>tin</term>; and in old, neglected <term>tin-works</term>, they find some of their <term>tools</term>. The workmen call them **the Saracens' refuse** <note>original: attal sarazin; "atal" is Cornish for "off-cast" or "refuse," though the author attributes it to "Hebrew speech"</note>, or the *Jews'* discarded works, in their *Hebrew* speech, says <term>Norden</term>. Now, I apprehend he means our <term>Arabians</term>, and it is a circumstance confirming my former notions. To this we may also refer the origin of the odd reports of our <term>Stonehenge</term> coming from <term>Africa</term> and the like. According to the *Greeks*, <term>Hercules</term>, and <term>Josephus</term>, <term>Hercules</term> is the son-in-law to <term>Aphricus</term>. The *Phoenicians* paid tithes; so did the <term>Arabians</term>, as noted in <term>Pliny</term>, these being <term>patriarchal customs</term>.

We may very well suppose <term>Aphricus</term> or <term>Phryxus</term> to be the father of the <term>Phrygians</term>. The *Greeks* color over his expedition through the <term>Propontis</term> <note>the Sea of Marmara</note> to the <term>Euxine Sea</term> <note>the Black Sea</note> with their stories of <term>Helle</term> and the <term>Hellespont</term>. But we cannot entertain too high a respect for him, because I see it is no less reasonable to refer the origin of the *Britons* to him. I mean that eastern colony that came here with <term>Hercules</term>, following the old possessors or original inhabitants, the <term>Albionites</term>, which gave the more famous name of <term>Britain</term> to the island. The <term>Brigantes</term> is the same name, says Mr. <term>Baxter</term>, and was the common and more ancient name of this people. Being driven northwards by floods of foreigners from the continent in later times, the name became more specifically applied to the inhabitants of <term>Yorkshire</term> and the neighboring counties. In the writings of <term>Tacitus</term>, the <term>Brigantes</term> are called **the greatest nation of the Britons** <note>original: maxima Brittanorum natio</note>. At the same time, they forced the oldest possessors, the <term>Albionites</term> or <term>Albanians</term>, even further northwards. Likewise, many of these <term>Brigantes</term> passed into <term>Ireland</term>, where they became a famous nation. The <term>Bryges</term>, <term>Phryges</term>, <term>Phrixi</term>, <term>Brisones</term>, <term>Brigantes</term>, and <term>Britones</term> are entirely synonymous words in different dialects. This assignment of the origin of our ancestors accounts very well for the notion of their <term>Phrygian</term> or <term>Trojan descent</term>, which was so firmly rooted in the minds of the old <term>Britons</term>. This was a notion that prevailed among some of the *Gallic nations* on the continent, and they had retained the memory of it in the time of <term>Ammianus Marcellinus</term>, who mentions it. Likewise, in <term>Caesar's</term> time, some *Gallic nations* claimed kinship with the <term>Romans</term>, probably for this very reason.

This is, in short, some presumptive evidence we have of <term>Hercules</term> and <term>Aphricus</term> planting the colony of <term>Britain</term>, introducing the <term>Druids</term> with the <term>patriarchal religion</term>, and sharing the knowledge they had of the use of the <term>compass</term>. This whole matter will be further considered when I come to treat of it specifically. At present, we will continue the history of the <term>compass</term> as it became more fully known to the world.

<term>Martinius</term> in his <term>Atlas</term>, and <term>Gilbert</term> in his book *On the Magnet* <note>original: Gilbertus de magnete</note> (Book 1, Chapter 2), show us that the <term>Chinese</term> have used the <term>magnetic needle</term> from time immemorial; they even have a trick of telling <term>fortunes</term> with it, just as the aforementioned heathens made it <term>oracular</term>. The <term>Arabians</term> likewise have used it for traveling over the great and wild <term>deserts</term> for weeks together, where there is no track to guide them; nor do they have any idea when they first began this practice. <term>Herwartius</term> published *The Marvels of Pagan Theology* <note>original: admiranda ethnicæ theologiæ</note>, wherein he endeavors to prove that the old <term>Egyptians</term> had the use of the <term>magnetic needle</term>, and that the <term>Bembin table</term> <note>An elaborate bronze tablet with Egyptian motifs, also called the Mensa Isiaca</note> contains the doctrine of it, enveloped in <term>hieroglyphicks</term>. The learned <term>Fuller</term> in his *Miscellanies* (Book 4, Chapter 19) asserts that the <term>Phoenicians</term> knew the use of it, which they endeavored to conceal by all possible means, as they did their trading in general. # STONEHENGE

with them, as many other arts, their *feather-working* <note>original: "ars plumaria"; the ancient art of making decorative items from feathers or fine embroidery</note>, the dyeing of purple (the invention of our *Hercules of Tyre* <note>The Phoenician god Melqart, often credited by the ancients with discovering the secret of Murex purple dye</note>), *Hebrew* poetry, and other curious knowledge, which is perished.

It is not unlikely that the <term>lodestone</term> <note>A naturally magnetized piece of the mineral magnetite, used as an early compass</note> being applied to religious use was one cause of its being forgotten, together with the secrecy of the *Phoenician* voyages. *Suetonius*, in his life of *Nero*, speaks of a prophetic needle which the emperor used to pay his devotions to. The learned *Burman* shows that most, or all of the old manuscripts and printed books, read it as *small needle* <note>original Latin: "acuncula", "acucula", or "acungula"</note>; which, in my opinion, the critics have needlessly corrected into *small image* <note>original Latin: "icuncula"</note>, because they had no idea that the magnetic needle was what was meant by it.

*Monsieur Fauchet*, a famous *French* antiquary, in his antiquities of *France*, quotes some verses from a poet in that country who wrote in 1180 A.D., wherein is as plain a description of the mariner’s box <note>a compass housing</note> as words can make. The poet mentions it by accident, not as a thing new and strange. *Osorius*, in his discourse of the acts of King *Emanuel*, refers the use of the compass among the *Europeans* to *Gama* and the *Portuguese*, who found it among some "barbarous" pirates about the *Cape of Good Hope*; who probably were some remains of the old *Phoenicians* or *Arabians*, or at least have preserved this practice from them. About 1260 A.D., *Paulus Venetus* <note>The Latin name for Marco Polo</note> is said to have brought it from *China*, according to the great author on the magnet, our countryman *Gilbert* <note>William Gilbert (1544–1603), physician to Elizabeth I and author of "De Magnete"</note>. *Genebrand*, in his chronology, says the use of the <term>lodestone</term> revived among us about 1303 A.D. by *Flavio Melvius*, a *Neapolitan*; others attribute it about that time to *John Goia*, also a *Neapolitan*. *Joseph de Costa* says some *Mahometan* seamen whom *Vasco da Gama* met with near *Mozambique*, who had sailed those seas by the use thereof, taught it to him.

I observe our ancient *Britons*, the *Welsh*, call a steersman or pilot *llwydd*, from which no doubt comes our *English* word <term>lodestone</term> and <term>lodestar</term> (the North Star). *Llyw* is the helm of a ship in the *British* language. <term>Lodemanage</term> in *Skinner’s* etymology is an old *English* word signifying the price paid to the pilot. Our lords of the *Cinque Ports* <note>A historic group of five port towns in southeast England—Hastings, New Romney, Hythe, Dover, and Sandwich—originally tasked with providing ships for the King</note> keep a court at *Dover* by that name. These things seem to indicate some memory of the magnet left among the *Welsh* from the oldest times, and of its application to sailing.

Thus have we given a kind of history of this prodigy in nature, the magnetic needle, to confirm our suspicion that the *British Druids* knew the use of it and used it in these works of theirs which we have been treating of. We learn in the *Philosophical Transactions* (Lowthorp, Vol. II, p. 601) that there are considerable veins of the magnet in our own country, in *Devonshire*, where the *Phoenicians* and *Druids* must have been very well acquainted.

We return now to our first subject, *Stonehenge*, and apply what has been said to the observation we there made. It is not to be thought that the *Druids*—men who employed themselves in those noble studies which *Caesar* gives us an account of, and who were at the pains of bringing these vast stones together from such a considerable distance of 16 miles—I say, it is not to be thought but that they would be careful and exact in placing them. And this, not only particularly in respect of each other upon the planned ground-plot, but also in general in respect of the quarters of the heavens. And this I found to be a correct guess when I examined their works for several years together with sufficient accuracy using a <term>theodolite</term> <note>A precision instrument used by surveyors to measure horizontal and vertical angles</note>. 

As I noted before, the works of one place followed the cardinal points <note>North, South, East, and West</note>, but with a certain uniform variation from them. From this I grounded my theory that they were set by a compass, which at that time varied according to the amount observed. We may well suppose the *Druids* were ignorant of this property of variation <note>The difference between true north and magnetic north, which changes over time</note>. This I now propose as the rule for investigating the time when *Stonehenge* was erected, hoping the reader will judge the attempt as favorably as things of this great antiquity require. The variation at *Stonehenge* is about 6 or 7 degrees from the north toward the east.
